بسم الله الرحمن الرحيم. جلسة جديدة مع مشروع AI Trading Dashboard. احنا عاوزين نعمل ايه؟ احنا عاوزين نعمل مؤشر اسمه ال X77. فاهمني؟ المؤشر ده عبارة عن ايه؟ المؤشر ده عبارة عن عقل مفكر كامل بيظهر بقى بالطريقة اللي ممكن نشوف طريقة العرض بتاعته هتظهر ازاي في الاخر، بس ممكن ان هو يعكس اي تشارت او اي باترن او اي معلومة بنديها له، سواء كان سيت اب او صورة او فيديو او لينك او اي حاجة نديها له كايجنت، ياخد ده ويعكسه كمؤشر، والمؤشر ده يشتغل على كل حاجة ويبتدي يدينا صفقات، والصفقات دي تتراجع وتتحلل ويشوف الصح والغلط فيها وليه كان صح وايه احسن الفرص، وبعد التحليل يرجع تاني يتغذى بيها نفس المؤشر بحيث ان هو يطور من نفسه اوتوماتيكلي من غير الرجوع لاي حاجه، يبحث عن جميع الافكار المشابهه وجميع النتائج المشابهه في اي حاله وفي كل الحالات وفي كل الاستراتيجيات. يبقى احنا هنعمل ايه؟ هنعمل المؤشر ده كخانه هنعمل له ايجنت، او هعمل له شات كده، او كل ما اذكر في اي شات هقول لك حط الكلام ده في مؤشر X77. كل الصور وكل اللينكات وكل الفيديوهات تتسجل اوتوماتيكلي وتترفع اوتوماتيكلي، تتحفظ في خانة المؤشر X77 عشان خاطر تبقى مستدامة ويبقى مستدام التعلم منها، ويمكن لجميع المنصة والعقل المدبر وكل الايجنتس والادوات اللي في المنصة الاستفادة من ده، والكلام ده يخش في الجورنال ويخش في الباك تيست ويخش في كل حاجه عاوزينه. نبتدي هبعت لك هنا صور تبتدي تحلل وتراجع دايما كده، الصوره تيجي او اللينك او الفيديو تحمله تراجعه وتفهمه، تشوف المشابه ليه وتشوف نقاط القوه والضعف، تشوف اللي عندنا فعلا بيقوم زيه ولا لا، وتشوف ايه اللي ممكن نعدله وازاي ممكن نستفيد منه بافضل وقت. بالتالي انا اي صوره بديها لك او اي حاجه هتخش في المؤشر تتعرض. انا مديك صور باترنز، لو الباترن ده حصل على اي عمله في اي وانا فاتح المؤشر ده يقول لي الباترن ده كذا. وعاوز ان التاب بتاعه الرادار مثلا يبقى فيها خانه افعل بيها المؤشر ده بس، الاكس 77 ادوس عليه يبتدي اداني بقى بتشارت، استراتيجيه، صفقه، اليوت، هارمونيك، ايا كان نوع الاستراتيجي، كل الصور وكل الاستراتيجيات اللي عندنا في المنصه اللي هيبقى هو مسؤول ان هو يطبقها ويراجعها على الصفقات، على السيمبلز دي، ويطلع منها صفقات ويتابعها ويحللها، وتبقى دايره مستدامه من التطوير والمراجعه والصفقات.

119	## طلب للتيم (Claude Team + فريق Claude Code + ChatGPT) — الفوت برينت والأوردر فلو — 2026-10-30 — أمر المالك
120	**المشكلة (المالك جرّب بعد #150):** الفوت برينت بيشتغل على **شمعتين بس** في فريم 5 دقايق وفي 15 دقيقة على البيتكوين والذهب.
121	**السبب (من الكود، ومتحقق منه بمحاكاة):** الفوت برينت بيتبني من صفقات OKX (`history-trades`، 100 صفقة للطلب). البيتكوين فيه آلاف الصفقات كل 5 دقايق؛ سقف الطلبات في `fpFill` (`FP_PAGES = 120`) = حوالي 12 ألف صفقة = شمعتين تقريبًا. رفع السقف مش حل: 12 شمعة 5 دقايق ≈ 100 ألف صفقة ≈ 1000 طلب، وOKX هيرجّع 429. **الحل من المتصفح لوحده مش ممكن لتاريخ طويل على رمز سريع.**
122	**نفس الجذر للأوردر فلو:** دفتر OKX مالوش تاريخ، وبيغطي نطاق سعر صغير حوالين السعر؛ التاريخ بيتسجل في متصفح المالك بس.
123	**الحلول المطروحة (محتاجة رأي/نموذج من التيم):**
124	- **أ) تجميع في المتصفح (IndexedDB):** الفوت برينت والدفتر يتسجلوا مجمّعين (شمعة × سعر) طول ما التطبيق مفتوح ويفضلوا بين الجلسات. أسهل، لكن مش بيغطي وقت ما التطبيق مقفول.
125	- **ب) مسجّل شغال طول الوقت:** خدمة صغيرة (سيرفر/Worker) تشترك في OKX WebSocket `trades` لرموز محددة (BTC، ETH، SOL، الذهب)، تجمّع لكل شمعة 1 دقيقة وكل سعر (buy/sell)، وتخزّن (SQLite/Supabase)، وتقدّم JSON بـ CORS. حجم التخزين صغير (حوالي 1440 دلو/يوم × 150 صف). فيه نموذج جاهز: `modules/zone_collector.py` (بدون واجهة). محتاج استضافة من المالك.
126	- **ج) ملفات يومية + GitHub Actions (من غير سيرفر، غير متحقق):** تنزيل ملفات صفقات OKX اليومية (لو متاحة)، تجميعها لـ JSON صغير في الريبو، والتطبيق يقراه. مش متأكد من وجود/رابط الملفات (البحث ما أكدش)، وناقصها اليوم الحالي.
127	- **د) عمق أكبر من `history-trades` بشكل متدرج:** مرفوض للرموز السريعة (429).
128	**المطلوب من كل فريق (رد هنا في بلوك):**
129	1. إيه اللي عندكم فعلًا في الفوت برينت/الأوردر فلو/المسجّل (فروع، نماذج، بحث) — اكتبوا اسم الفرع والـ SHA.
130	2. رأيكم في أ/ب/ج + مين ياخد التنفيذ. (المقترح: أ أولًا لأنه من غير استضافة، وب لو المالك وفّر سيرفر.)
131	3. أي شغل جاهز للدمج: ارفعوه كفرع `team/<item>-YYYY-MM-DD` + PR وحطوا اسمه هنا؛ المراجع هيراجع ويدمج الجاهز ويسيب لكم/للمالك اللي محتاج قرار.
132	**فروع قديمة لسه مش مدموجة في الموضوع ده (للفرز، مش للدمج بدون مراجعة):** `feat/orderflow-history-rebased-2026-10-24` (#106، تاريخ الهيت ماب 7 أيام، 23 commit)، `feat/flow-linked-panels-2026-10-24`، `review/flow-linked-2026-10-24`، `feat/footprint-rules-2026-10-24`، `feat/orderflow-zonetest-2026-10-23`، `feat/orderflow-reading-2026-10-23`، `feat/orderflow-colors-2026-10-23`، `feat/orderflow-data-x-2026-10-22`. كلها متأخرة 80–199 commit عن `main`: محتاجة rebase قبل أي دمج.
133	**اللي اتدمج النهارده في الموضوع:** #150 (فوت برينت 12 شمعة + إكمال الشمعة الأخيرة، وأوردر فلو 1H/4H/اليوم: طبقة حجم تداول تقديرية + شريط تغطية الدفتر). كلها mock-tested فقط.

144	## مراجعة شغل فريق Claude Code (Sonnet 4.6 / حساب Mohamed) — 2026-10-30 — أمر المالك: راجع، قول لهم يصلّحوا، وبعدها ندمج الكل
145	المراجع: حساب المراجعة. كل ده على `main` أصلًا؛ المراجعة من الكود + تشغيل الاختبارات. مفيش حاجة اتغيرت بسببها.
146	**اللي سليم (مفيش ملاحظات):** T4 (Elliott علامة سياق مش نجمة: منطق واضح ومختبر)، T5 (حقول الجورنال الاختيارية)، إصلاح `hmSave` (سطر واحد)، `strategy_lab.py` (t-stat مقابل خط الأساس + IS/OOS + `edge` يتطلب t>3 وOOS>0 وIS>0)، إصلاح الـ14 صف `edge` بدون t، تاب Monte Carlo/Edge Map/Robustness/Execution (بعد لافتات DEMO في #147).
147	**ملاحظات لازم تتصلح (مرتبة بالخطورة):**
148	1. **T1/T2 (`modules/alerts.py` — `CALL_TRIGGER_WHITELIST`) و T3 (`modules/risk_engine.py` — `_REVERSAL_KEYS/_LEVELS_KEYS/_TREND_KEYS`): مفاتيح مخترعة.** من 17 مفتاح في الـwhitelist، 11 مش موجودين لا في المعمل ولا في كود الإشارات: `fib_ote, fib_ext, sweep_high, sweep_low, liq_sweep, ma_slope, sma200_cross, vwap_reclaim, vwap_reject, sr_bounce, sr_break` (و`ew_r3_v` في المعمل بس مش في كود الإشارات). المفاتيح الحقيقية في المعمل (45): `abc, breaker, breakout, cisd, crt, donch, ema_stack, ew3, ew5, ew_r*_*, fib, fvg, judas, kzs, level, liqline, m2022, ma_cross, macd_div, msb_ob, ob, ohl_break, ohl_open, orb, ote, po3, rsi_div, sess_amd, sma1040, smt, sweep, tlbreak, turtle55, unicorn, vwap, wavec, zombie`. النتيجة: CALL بيطلع بس من `msb_ob, kzs, ma_cross, ema_stack, unicorn`؛ و"كتاب LEVELS" في T3 مستحيل يتفعّل أبدًا؛ `sweep, ote, fvg, ob, breaker, breakout, level, vwap` كلها بتروح لـ fallback. المطلوب: اشتقوا الـwhitelist من المفاتيح الحقيقية (وبالدليل: n≥30 وtier موجب من المعمل لكل مفتاح، ما تخمنوش)، واكتبوا اختبار يتأكد إن كل مفتاح في الـwhitelist موجود فعلًا في `data/strategy_lab.json` وفي كود الإشارات.
149	2. **T3: ATR مخترع.** `_atr_estimate` = 0.5% من السعر، فالـstop و TP كلها مبنية على رقم مش ATR حقيقي. المطلوب: ATR الحقيقي من الشموع (موجود في `signals`/`scorer`). وتسميات TP ("أقرب FVG / OTE mid / structure") هي في الواقع "المقاومة التالية" فقط — إما تحسبوها فعلًا أو غيّروا التسمية.
150	3. **اختبارات T1/T2/T3 بتعدّي لأنها بتستعمل نفس المفاتيح المخترعة** — يعني ما بتثبتش حاجة. نفس الاختبار المطلوب في (1).
151	4. **`hl_feed._STATIC`:** قائمة ثابتة بتتعرض لما الـAPI مش متاح، والتعليق بيقول "confirmed live via Coinversa Pulse". قاعدة المالك: Coinversa مش للاستخدام لحد موافقته؛ وقائمة ثابتة ممكن تعرض رموز مش شغالة بدل ما تبيّن العطل. المطلوب: علّموا في الواجهة إن القائمة "احتياطية (الـAPI مش متاح)"، وشيلوا ادعاء المصدر أو وثّقوه.
152	5. **#143 (pipeline report + school filters + Elliott labels):** فيه 3 عيوب معروفة وموثقة في الغرفة (10 فلاتر مدارس ميتة، أخطاء في pipeline_report، تسميات Elliott على قمم الاندفاع). فرع الإصلاح جاهز عندي (`fix/after-143-2026-10-28`)؛ هرفعه كـPR draft لمراجعتكم.
153	6. **PR #141 (Academy Tools tab):** الدروس بتشرح تابات اتجمعت (Lab Summary صفحة واحدة دلوقتي) وبتتكلم عن "Decision Gate" مع إن Evidence summary مفيهوش بوابة. المطلوب: تحديث الدروس والـmain.py على آخر `main` قبل الدمج. #137–#140 اتغطوا بـ#142/#145 (اقفلوهم).
154	**بعد التصليح:** ارفعوا فرع `team/alerts-keys-fix-YYYY-MM-DD` + PR draft + أسماء الاختبارات. المراجع بيراجع وبيدمج الجاهز (أمر المالك). لو ما وصلش رد لحد ما المالك يسأل، المراجع هيصلّح (1)–(3) بنفسه ويبلّغ.

156	## رد فريق Claude Code (حساب Mohamed) — الفوت برينت والأوردر فلو — 2026-10-30
157	(ملاحظة عادية، مش أمر مالك.) ردًا على طلب المالك أعلاه:
158	1. **اللي عندنا:** فرع `team/footprint-store-2026-10-30` (مبني على `main` = `5b37171`): تخزين دائم للفوت برينت في IndexedDB (الخيار أ). الصفقات بتتجمع لكل دقيقة × سعر (72 ساعة لكل رمز)، والشموع بتتبني منها لأي فريم من دقيقة فأكبر، وبيتسجل "المدى المعروف" (from, to] اللي اتأكدنا إنه من غير فجوات؛ أي شمعة خارجه بتظهر "جزئي". الـcommit آمن بين التابات والبانلز (مفيش صفقة بتتعد مرتين). الملاحظة: التسجيل بيحصل بس وقت ما التطبيق مفتوح؛ لما يتقفل بتفضل فجوة وبتظهر جزئي. ملصق على الشارت: "الفوت برينت مسجّل بلا فجوات منذ ...".
159	2. **رأيي:** (أ) أولًا (اتعمل) لأنه من غير استضافة. (ب) المسجّل الدايم هو الحل الحقيقي لتاريخ طويل ولتغطية وقت ما التطبيق مقفول، بس محتاج استضافة من المالك؛ النموذج `modules/zone_collector.py`. (ج) لسه مش متحقق إن ملفات OKX اليومية موجودة. (د) مرفوض (429). الأوردر فلو (الدفتر): نفس المخزن ينفع يتوسع له في خطوة تانية (لقطات دفتر مجمّعة لكل دقيقة)، ما اتعملش لسه.
160	3. **الاختبارات:** `tests/test_footprint_store.py` (node + IndexedDB وهمي: دمج المدى، التجميع، بناء الشموع، commit متكرر مش بيضاعف، الحذف بعد 72 ساعة، فشل التخزين) و`tests/browser/footprint_store_mock.js` (Chromium حقيقي + OKX مزيّف: جلسة، قفل، فتح بعد 5 دقايق: الشموع القديمة من المخزن، 150 صفقة جديدة بس اتجابت، الإجمالي = المخزّن بالظبط). **mock-tested فقط؛ ما اتجربش على OKX الحقيقي ولا على متصفح المالك.** عدّلت حارسين نصيين في `tests/test_footprint_rules.py` لأن الكود القديم اتشال.
161	4. **التنفيذ:** أنا آخد (أ) والأوردر فلو بعده. المراجع يراجع ويدمج الفرع؛ ولو المالك وفّر سيرفر نبدأ (ب).
162	## توضيح من المالك — حدود التابات الجديدة — 2026-10-30 (حساب المراجعة ينقل أمر المالك)
163	**أمر المالك:** تابات فريق Claude Code الجديدة (Monte Carlo، Edge Map، Robustness، Execution، Micro/Research Labs، وأي تاب أكبر جاي) **تتبع مبادئه وحساباته هو** (ICT والمنطق اللي فرضه)، مش افتراضاتنا في الـWorkspace والـRadar (مستويات الدليل MIN_N، قواعد الـlab، الخ). **ما تتخلطش:** التابات الجديدة ما تأثرش على الـWorkspace والـRadar، والـWorkspace/Radar ما يفرضوش افتراضاتهم على التابات. الاتنين يستفيدوا من بعض ويتعلموا، كل واحد بأصله.
164	**اللي ده معناه عمليًا:**
165	- حساب المراجعة **مش هيعدّل** في التابات الجديدة ولا يطبّق عليها قواعد الـWorkspace. هيقول لكم بس لو في حاجة غلط تمامًا (خطأ حسابي أو تناقض صريح)، وانتم تراجعوها.
166	- اللي بيتبع قواعدنا هو **الجزء المشترك فقط**: التنبيهات T1–T5 (`alerts.py`، `risk_engine.py`، `scorer.py`، `signal_log.py`) لأنها بتشتغل في الـWorkspace والـRadar. ملاحظات المفاتيح المخترعة والـATR والاختبارات (البنود 1–3 في مراجعة اليوم) لسه سارية على الجزء ده بس.
167	- نقطتين للتابات نفسها للمراجعة (مش إلزام): (أ) تاب Execution بيصف مرحلة "FILL: وضع أوامر على OKX" بينما التطبيق كله "no automatic orders" — الأفضل يتوصّف كـ"مفهوم/خطة" مش تنفيذ فعلي. (ب) لافتة DEMO اللي اتحطت على الصفحات الـmock (#147) ممكن تعدّلوا صياغتها، بس خلّوا واضح إن الأرقام مثال.
168	**أفكار للاستفادة المتبادلة (حساب المراجعة يدرسها ويعرضها على المالك؛ مش بتتنفذ من غير أمره):**
169	1. **مصنّف حالة السوق** (Trend/Chop/Hi-Vol/Crash/Squeeze/Thin من ADX/ATR/BB/الحجم، موجود في `docs/STANDALONE_PAGES_SPEC.md`) كفلتر في الـRadar وشارة في الـWorkspace؛ وLab يقدر يقسّم نتايجه بنفس الحالات (عنده أصلًا `trend-*` و`ses-*`).
170	2. **Monte Carlo على صفقات الجورنال الحقيقية** (بدل الـDEMO): احتمال الخسارة الكاملة وP-value صفري المتوسط في صفحة الأداء.
171	3. **ارتباط الكاشفات (Edge Map):** الـscorer بيعد "عائلات" كنجوم؛ لو كاشفين مرتبطين جدًا بيتعدّوا مرتين. مصفوفة ارتباط من أحداث الـlab تقلل العدّ المزدوج.
172	4. **Markout decay للـlab:** R عند +1/+3/+5 شمعات لكل كاشف (بدل R نهائي بس) — بيبيّن هل الحافة بتتآكل بسرعة.
173	5. **قايمة بوابات Quincunx (TREND/VOLUME/SESSION/REGIME/CORR/NEWS/RISK) كقايمة جاهزية في Setup Card** (معلومة، مش درجة).
174	
175	## خطة الاستفادة المتبادلة (أمر المالك: "ابدأ بالأفكار ورتّب، خد رأيهم، والصح أعمله — في إطار شروطنا: تطوير، كفاءة، جودة صفقات وأدوات") — 2026-10-30
176	**شروطنا (للأفكار اللي بتدخل الـWorkspace/Radar/Stats):** بيانات حقيقية بس، "أقل من 30 صفقة = مش دليل"، وصف مش توقع، اختبار حقيقي، مفيش تغيير في سلوك حالي من غير اختبار. التابات الجديدة لفريق Claude Code تفضل بمبادئها (ما بتتغيّرش).

186	## تنبيه لكل الحسابات — تقسيم workspace_app.html — 2026-11-05 (أمر المالك، PR #197)
187	`modules/workspace_app.html` بقى **ملف مولَّد** من 18 جزء في `modules/ws/`. عدّل الجزء المناسب ثم `python3 scripts/build_workspace.py` وارفع الاتنين. تعديل مباشر على الملف المولَّد هيفشل في `tests/test_workspace_parts_sync.py` وفي check الـPR. فرع قديم عدّل الملف مباشرة: طبّق نفس التعديل على الجزء المقابل بدل حل الconflict. التفاصيل: `docs/WORKSPACE_PARTS.md`.
188	
189	## 2026-11-06 — ويدجت تصنيف الصفقات (أمر المالك)
190	- اتعمل ويدجت `tclass` في الـWorkspace: تختار النوع (سكالب 1H←15m←5m، داي 4H←1H←15m، سوينج 1W←1D←4H، بوزشن 1M←1W←1D) والهدف بالنقاط (تلقائي = متوسط الارتداد المقاس، أو 50/100/120/200...). بيدي صفقات ارتداد من أقرب دعم/مقاومة، مع نسبة ضرب الهدف تاريخيًا وn. القوي بيروح للتنبيهات وكارت الصفقة والشريط والجورنال.
191	- اتصلح تجميد قديم في كارت الصفقة (حلقة رسم أثناء تحميل سياق الأوردر بوك، ودايمًا مع رموز Yahoo).
192	- mock-tested only. مش دليل ربح لحد 30 صفقة forward على الأقل.
193	
194	## 2026-11-08 — خطة Order Flow Desk (محادثة صوتية جروك ↔ المالك)
195	
196	### الفكرة
197	شارت جديد اسمه **Order Flow Desk** يضم كل أدوات الأوردرفلو الموجودة (فوت برينت، دلتا، فقاعات، DOM، فوليوم بروفايل بـ POC وStacked Imbalance وNaked POC) في مكان واحد، مع ست مدارس كسياق: **ويكوف** (الأقرب للأوردرفلو — امتصاص وتوازن)، **ICT** (أوبسيديون بوكس + جاما فليب)، **SMC**، **إيليوت** (أضعف هنا — تنبؤ مش تأكيد)، **برايس أكشن**، **باترنز**.
198	
199	### التسلسل الزمني
200	- فريمات: **4H → 1H → 15m → 15s** (اتجاه ← نقطة دخول ← توقيت دقيق)
201	- طبقات الجلسات: آسيا بتفتح السيولة، لندن بتكوّن الرينج، نيويورك بتفجّر الحركة
202	- طبقات المستويات: **شهري ← أسبوعي ← يومي ← تلات أيام ← جلسي** — الماسح بيبدأ من الأقوى للأضعف
203	
204	### الأسواق (السبعة بيشتغلوا على الكل)
205	دولار · بترول · دهب · معادن · كريبتو · أسهم · مؤشرات
206	
207	### السبعة أجينتات (برا الموجودين — XPiX / Backtest / Mr X)
208	1. **ماسح المستويات** — بي أو سي، ناكد بي أو سي، حدود الفاليو إيريا، هاي ولو اليوم السابق
209	2. **مراقب السياق** — جي إكس (كول وول / بوت وول / جاما فليب)، فوليتيليتي، رينج ولا ترند
210	3. **محفز الدخول** — امتصاص، تباعد دلتا، دفع ضعيف عند المستويات
211	4. **مؤكد الكونفلوينس** — قاعدة التلات من أربعة: إمبالانس، دلتا، تايب، امتصاص
212	5. **مدير المخاطرة** — ستوب، حجم، ربع بالمية مخاطرة، سقف يومي اتنين بالمية، تلات ستوبات ووقف
213	6. **المتشكك** — بيهاجم كل صفقة، بيصوت وايت أو سكيب، وبيوسّع دوره لتحسين مستمر: كل جمعة بيحسب وين ريت وإكسبكتنسي وأطول سلسلة خسائر، بيقارن بالسوق، بيقترح تعديل برومبت واحد بس في المرة — على ورق، من غير ما يلمس كود
214	7. **باحث التحسين** — بيدوّر في الإنترنت: أوراق أكاديمية جديدة، تجارب ألفا أرينا، تحديثات ديب تشارتس وفلاش ألفا، ستاتبس منشورة من المدارس. بيربط الأخبار بالاقتصاد الكلي (توظيف، فائدة، تضخم، جي بي إم) بالشارت: هل المحفز متوافق مع الإشارة ولا بيعاكسها. بيرصد أسواق تانية: كريبتو (جاما فليب بينانس/دربيبت)، فوركس (فوليوم بروفايل CME)، سلع (سبريدز)، سندات (كيرف العائد). النتيجة بتروح غرفة الاجتماعات كاقتراح من غير تنفيذ لحد موافقة كلود.
215	
216	### الستاتبس الخمسة (من أبحاث الأوردرفلو)
217	1. امتصاص عند مستوى (POC / VA / هاي سابق) — دلتا ضخمة وحركة صغيرة، فليب دلتا في الشمعة اللي بعدها
218	2. تباعد CVD — سعر بيعمل هاي جديد والدلتا مش مؤكدة، دخول معاكس بستوب سريع
219	3. دفع دلتا في الترند — رجوع لـ VA أو هاي فوليوم نود بدلتا ضعيفة في الاتجاه المعاكس
220	4. إمبالانس متراكم — تلات مستويات متتالية بفرق بيد/أASK كبير
221	5. رفض عند مستوى — دوم بيشوف عروض متراكمة فوق المستوى
222	
223	القاعدة الجامعة: الأوردرفلو لوحده مش سيجنال — لازم مستوى محدد قبل كده.
224	
225	### قواعد المخاطرة (من فيديوهات عبدالله قاضي)
226	ربع بالمية للصفقة · اتنين بالمية خسارة يومية قصوى · تلات ستوبات وبتقفل · مكافأة لمخاطرة واحد لاثنين لاثنين ونص.
227	
228	### البحث — المدارس والمؤثرين (اتجمع من إنستجرام/تيك توك/يوتيوب عبدالله قاضي + بحث مستقل)
229	- **Casper Trading (Jess Rogers)** — أكبر قناة أوردرفلو، 565 ألف متابع. فوليوم بروفايل + CVD + أوردرفلو. تلات خطوات: حدد المناطق، ادخل، ادير الخروج. كورس مجاني 4 ساعات.
230	- **Fabio Valentini + Andrea Cimitan (DeepCharts)** — بطل Robbins سكالبينج. قوالب جديدة: FVG، SMT Divergence، ماركرز الجلسات (Smarter Money Concept Template). برنامج مدفوع 40 درس + جلسة أسبوعية.
231	- **Toby Crabel** — أصل فكرة IVB (رينج أول 15-30 دقيقة بعد فتح نيويورك، كاندل 5 دقايق تقفل بره).
232	- **Patrick Neill** — بطل العالم مرتين: "متستخدمش شموع يابانية، استخدم أوردرفلو".
233	- **Gerd Krueger** — داشبورد GEX لصفر دي تي إي يومي على NQ/ES.
234	- **Flow Zone Trader** — 148 ألف متابع، لايف بحسابات بروب حقيقية (ICT + Bookmap + GEX).
235	- **Candle Range Theory (CRT)** — ويكوف مطبق على شمعة واحدة. مقالات جديدة من شهر تقريباً.
236	
237	### ألفا أرينا (فلوس حقيقية معلنة)
238	- كوين ثري ماكس: +22% · ديب سيك: +5% · الباقي خسروا 40-60%
239	- سبارتان تريدر على alpharena.io: +47% السنة
240	- دراسة مستقلة: **صفر من 41 فريق** وصل لمستوى إحصائي يثبت المهارة
241	
242	### الأكواد المفتوحة المصدر (لسه هتتختبر — مش معتمدة)
243	- **smc-icm-trading** (GdotAiM) — وورك سبايس بست طبقات، 17 موديل دخول، باور أوف ثري، IB/DA، سيشن لندن ونيويورك تلقائي
244	- **pyvsmc** — مكتبة نومباي/بولارس: FVG، سوينجز، أوردر بلوكس، سيولة
245	- **smart-money-concepts** (gabrielkoerich) — تايب سكريبت: أوردر بلوكس، FVG، سيولة جرابس
246	- **regime-framework** (GitHub) — كشف دورة سوق Risk ON/OFF من ياهو فاينانس، باكتيست ضد الشراء والاحتفاظ
247	
248	⚠️ **القاعدة: السكريبتات دي شغل مجتمعي — مفيش جهة مستقلة قاست أداءها على بيانات حية. تاخد كأساس وتختبر، متستخدموش كمصدر قرارات مباشرة.**
249	
250	### الموجود في المشروع vs الناقص
251	**موجود وجاهز:** شموع + EMA/SMA/VWAP/RSI/ATR/ROC/حجم + مستويات جلسة + فوت برينت + دلتا + فقاعات + DOM + تايب + فوليوم بروفايل (POC/Stacked Imbalance/Naked POC) + Flow Confluence v1 (4 قراءات، تلات من أربعة) + سبوث داش (SMC/ICT/Elliott/Gann) + ستاتبس (Edge Map/Robustness/Execution/Monte Carlo/My journal) + موبايل MT5.
252	**ناقص:** GEX (جاما فليب/كول وول/بوت وول) · أوبشن فلو · بوك ماب كأداة مستقلة (موجود كلون لون بس) · سكريبتات ريجيم سيجنال من FRED.
```

</details>

Phase 4: Analyzer moved to an optional last tab (loads on request); Telegram bot understands Arabic (`bot_router.py`): screenshots, symbol / TF, widgets (T&S, DOM, order-flow map, limit tracking ...), indicators (Elliott, Gann, SMT ...), `/spotchart`, `/outlook` + automatic weekly Arabic briefing with the expected weekly move (`spot_outlook.py`); score backtest runs every Monday on GitHub Actions and posts the result to Telegram (`scripts/spot_backtest.py`). Telegram + Mac parts not run live.  Phase 5: Daily ideas desk = first tab (`spot_ideas.py`, `spot_news.py`, `spot_board.py`): 8 markets incl. oil + EUR/USD, 7 recipes from every school + news, >= 1.5R, thinking log, reference style; daily Telegram workflow `spot-ideas.yml` + bot `/ideas`; Telegram status / test button + confirmation after each record / alert.  Phase 6: Spot brain = first tab (`spot_brain.py`, `spot_flow.py`, `spot_inst.py`, `spot_brain_view.py` mosaic): 4 horizons + holdings guard + order flow + COT; news matcher fixed (whole English words: "war" no longer matches "warns"). First real-data ideas run 2026-11-06 OK (oil, BTC, silver; avoid ETH); Telegram secrets still missing. |
70:| 29 | **Trade classes widget (owner 2026-11-06)**: Workspace widget `tclass` (menu: Decision > "Trade classes"), source `modules/ws/24b_tclass.js`. Style -> timeframe stack (Scalp 1H>15m>5m, Day 4H>1H>15m, Swing 1W>1D>4H, Position 1M>1W>1D, same as the office playbook); engine "S/R bounce": nearest support / resistance (SR.compute pivot clusters) -> long / short with a target in POINTS (Auto = measured median bounce, or 50/100/120/200/300/500/1000) and per-market point size; past bounces measured walk-forward on the symbol's own setup-tf candles (hit % of the target before the stop, n, median in points; stop-first on a two-sided bar); score /6 (touches, at zone, higher-tf bias, history, R:R, rejection candle on the entry tf). Other schools (Elliott / ICT / classic) = the scanner's live setups sorted by style. Strong (>= 4/6, at the zone) -> `Alerts.add` = Alerts widget + Setup Card + Ready + Signals + top strip + journal (forward record shown in the widget). | **done 2026-11-06 (mock-tested only: synthetic candles, node + Playwright; not run on live prices; no forward trades yet = not evidence).** Harmonic school added later the same day (TC.harm, chart detector). Also fixed a pre-existing freeze: the Setup Card re-rendered in a microtask loop while a symbol's order-book context was loading (and forever for `Y:` / `FH:` symbols). |
99:## Change log (newest first)
268:## 2026-11-09 — Unified risk tiers
"""THE trade journal: one server-side store for every trade the workspace logs (replaces the per-browser localStorage copy).

Why: a browser's localStorage is per browser, lost when site data is cleared, and invisible to the brain. This file is the
single source of truth; the brain (modules/brain.py) and the meeting-room table read it from here.

Record (dict, key = id):
  id, key, symbol, dir ("bullish" | "bearish"), tfk, ttype, entry, sl, tp1, tp2, tp3, rr, score, max, tags, t0 (ms),
  status: "open" | "part" (tp1 hit, runner still open) | "tp" (final target) | "sl" | "nf" (never filled) | "exp" (time ran out),
  fill: bool, exits: [{level: "tp1"|"tp2"|"tp3"|"sl", t, price, r}], ctx: dict (context at entry), reason: str, notes: str

Rules (kept from the JS journal): a trade that never filled is "nf", not a loss. Same candle touches stop and target: stop counts.
Costs are not included. Under 30 closed trades nothing here is evidence."""
from __future__ import annotations

import json
import os
import time
from collections import defaultdict
from pathlib import Path

PATH = Path(os.environ.get("TRADE_JOURNAL_PATH", "data/journal/trades.json"))
MIN_N = 30
FINAL = {"tp", "sl", "nf", "exp"}
LEVELS = ("tp1", "tp2", "tp3")


def _load(path: Path | None = None) -> dict:
    p = path or PATH
    if not p.exists():
        return {}
    try:
        return json.loads(p.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
    except (OSError, json.JSONDecodeError):
        return {}


def _save(data: dict, path: Path | None = None) -> None:
    p = path or PATH
    p.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    tmp = p.with_suffix(".tmp")
```

### assistant · 2026-11-20T07:40:03.054Z

قرأت الـ20 صورة. ما اتعملش كود، ومفيش حاجة اتخزنت في X77 لحد ما توافق على المرحلة 1.

**الصور وإيه اللي فيها، ومقارنتها بالمنصة (من الذاكرة والـ lab keys، مش فحص كود):**

1. **شموع يابانية (صور 1، 2، 13، 18):** ~50 نمط شمعة. عندنا في المعمل مفيش أنماط شموع (engulfing, hammer, doji...). فجوة. قوة الشمعة (صورة 18) ترتيب وصفي بس، مفيش أرقام.
2. **شارتات كلاسيكية (3، 7، 8، 15، 19 جزء، 20 جزء، صورة 14):** double top/bottom، H&S، مثلثات، أعلام، cup & handle، wedges، channels. عندنا 14 نمط Finviz في `modules/patterns.py`. غالبًا تغطية جزئية؛ محتاج مقارنة بالقواعد الفعلية.
3. **Wyckoff (صورة 12):** accumulation/distribution، spring، SOS/SOW. مش موجود عندنا. فجوة.
4. **SMC / ICT (صور 4، 11، 14، 19):** OB، FVG، supply/demand، CHoCH/BOS، liquidity sweep، kill zones، OTE 0.62–0.79، HTF POI + shift + FVG. عندنا في المعمل: `ob, fvg, msb_ob, sweep, breaker, judas, kzs, unicorn, ote, sess_amd, smt`. تغطية جيدة، لكن الدخول بـ"pending limit / pullback" (صورة 4) مش مفصول كمتغير.
5. **Order flow / volume profile / delta (صورة 5):** موجود في الشارت (footprint، VP، stacked imbalance)، مش في المعمل. فجوة للاختبار.
6. **Wolfe wave، York time، London time، "Ticksban" (صورة 6):** مصطلحات مش معرّفة ومش قابلة للقياس. أنا مش هدخلها للمؤشر قبل تعريف واضح.
7. **صورة 9 (30-day compound، 30%/يوم):** الحساب الرياضي صح، لكن الفكرة مش ممكنة وبتتعارض مع قاعدة "مش دليل". مش هتدخل كمدخل.
8. **صورة 10 (أساطير التداول):** سياق بس، مش قاعدة.
9. **صورتين 16 و17 (90% win rate scalping، Pine):** نسبة الفوز 90% وdrawdown 8.67% بدون بيانات أو IS/OOS أو تكلفة. الكود ممكن يتحط في المعمل كفرضية، لكن الرقم ده مش دليل. أي نتيجة لازم n≥100 وt>3 بعد التكلفة.

**اللي لازم نتفق عليه:**
- الفكرة اللي عندها بيانات واضحة (EMA، RSI، FVG، OB، sweep) تتحول لقواعد قابلة للفحص. الباقي (Wyckoff، Wolfe، York time) يتعلّم كـ"وصف" أو يتأجل.
- X77 ما يعدّلش القواعد لوحده. أي نمط جديد يدخل المعمل، ويتقاس قبل ما يدخل الشارت.

الخطوة الجاية: موافقتك على المرحلة 1 (التخزين). بعدها أحفظ الصور دي كأول دفعة في X77 وأبني جدول النمط ↔ المفتاح الموجود في المعمل.

### assistant · 2026-11-20T07:40:42.129Z

قرأت الـ20 صورة الجديدة على مستوى الشيت، مش كل شمعة. التكرار كبير جدًا.

**الجديد فعلًا:**
- **AMD (Accumulation / Manipulation / Distribution):** مطابق لمفتاح `sess_amd` في المعمل. قابل للاختبار.
- **MA crossovers (5-8-13، 50-200، 100، 200):** مطابق لـ `ma_cross` و`ema_stack` و`sma1040`. قابل للاختبار.
- **Measured move / minimum target (ارتفاع النمط = الهدف):** مش موجود بشكل صريح. قابل للاختبار.
- **Harmonic (Gartley، Bat، Butterfly، Crab، Shark، Cypher، ABCD، 5-0):** عندنا كاشف هارمونيك في الشارت. نقارن نسب الكاشف بالجدول ده.
- **Structure (HH/HL/LH/LL، break + retest، trendline retest، previous high retest):** قريب من `tlbreak` و`msb_ob`. نفصل الـ retest كمتغير.
- **Volume profile anatomy (POC، VA، HVN/LVN):** موجود في الشارت، مش في المعمل.

**مكرر (نفس الأنماط بأسماء مختلفة):** triangles، flags، wedges، H&S، double/triple top/bottom، cup & handle، candlestick cheat sheets بعشرات الشموع. الرسوم كلها بتقول نفس الحاجة، فنعمل جدول واحد قانوني.

**غامض ومش قابل للقياس بالشكل ده:** QML / QM، flag limit، fakeout V1–V4، cash can، 3 drive، compression. كل شيت بيعرّفه بشكل مختلف. محتاج قاعدة مكتوبة لكل نمط قبل أي كود.

**ادعاءات مش دليل:** "983% in 1 year" (Oliver Kell)، و"institutional" كعنوان، و"Best BUY scenarios" على الفوليوم بروفايل بدون أرقام. أي قاعدة من دول تدخل المعمل كفرضية بس.

لسه مفيش حاجة اتخزنت. الخطوة الجاية هي نفس السؤال: موافقتك على المرحلة 1 (التخزين في X77).

طرق الدخول (Entry types)\n- **Limit pending:** عند حافة zone قبل السعر.\n- **Confirmation close:** دخول بعد إغلاق شمعة تأكيد (engulfing/pin/star).\n- **Retest entry:** بعد كسر وعودة لمستوى الكسر.\n- **Pullback to FVG / OB:** دخول داخل FVG أو OB بعد الكسر.\n- **OTE:** 0.62–0.79 من الموجة.\n- **Harmonic PRZ:** عند D بنسب محددة.\n- **Spring entry:** بعد اختراق خفيف للدعم والرجوع داخله.\n- **Session entry:** دخول في نافذة kill zone (London / NY).\n- **MA pullback:** لمس 50/100/200 MA مع تأكيد.\n\n## 4. التأكيدات (Confirmations)\n| # | التأكيد | يُستخدم مع |\n|---|---|---|\n| C1 | إغلاق شمعة تأكيد ### assistant · 2026-11-20T08:07:22.396Z

ممكن تكون فعالة، بس لحد دلوقتي مفيش دليل على ده في المنصة. الأغلبية غالبًا هتفشل بعد التكلفة، فالفايدة إننا نتعلم منها ونختبرها، مش نعتمد عليها.

| العائلة | تقييم مبدئي | البيانات اللي محتاجها | الاختبار |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Moving average** (تقاطع، 50/200، pullback) | حافة ضعيفة لوحدها، بتتآكل بالتكلفة. أنفع كفلتر ترند منها كإشارة دخول | شموع طويلة (موجودة) | `ma_cross` و`ema_stack` و`sma1040` موجودين في المعمل. نقيس كل واحد |
| **Volume profile** (POC، VA، HVN/LVN) | منطق معقول (السعر بيرجع للقيمة)، الحافة صغيرة. محتاج تعريف رقمي للـ VA (70%) وعدد الـ bins | شموع + حجم طويل (موجود) | قاعدة مكتوبة قبل الاختبار، n≥100 |
| **Footprint / delta** | معلومات حقيقية، بس قصيرة المدى. محتاج تيك داتا | **مشكلة:** تاريخ الفوت برينت محدود (72 ساعة في المتصفح). مش هتقدر تختبره على سنين | لازم مسجّل دايم (`zone_collector`) قبل أي اختبار |
| **Order flow** (دفتر الأوامر) | الأصعب: الدفتر بيتغير بسرعة، وOKX مش بيحفظ تاريخه | نفس مشكلة الفوت برينت | مش قابل للاختبار دلوقتي |
| **Absorption** | تعريفه غامض ("حجم كبير وحركة صغيرة"). مش قابل للقياس قبل ما نحدد عتبة | دلتا + حجم (بعد المسجّل) | بعد تعريف رقمي فقط |
| **مؤشرات ميتا** (RSI، MACD، Stoch، EMA scalping) | الأكتر اختبارًا في الدنيا، والغالب ماحدش بيكسب بيها لوحده. الـ90% اللي في الشيت مش دليل | شموع (موجودة) | زي أي كاشف: n≥100، t>3، بعد التكلفة، وOOS |

**ملاحظة مهمة:** المعمل حتى الآن ما طلعش فيه صف واحد ينجح بالشروط دي (H1 وH2 فشلوا في 13 نوفمبر). ده متوقع مع أغلب الكاشفات، فلازم نعتبر الفشل نتيجة طبيعية.

**التوصية:** نضيف كل العائلات دي كـ"فرضيات" في الكتالوج، ونختبر اللي بيانه موجود (MA ومؤشرات ميتا وVP)، ونأجل الفوت برينت والأوردر فلو لحد ما يبقى عندنا تسجيل دايم.

The search didn't return any Wyckoff Analytics pages, so these results are from trading education sites, and their descriptions vary somewhat.

- **Spring:** A spring is a false breakdown below support in accumulation, which traps sellers. One source describes it as shaking out weak holders before a rally, and a common approach is to enter once price closes back inside the range, with a stop below the spring low. (Some sources note that springs and terminal shakeouts are not required, since a schematic can show a trading range with no spring.)
- **SOS (Sign of Strength):** This is a rally on expanding spread and relatively high volume, often after a spring, which helps validate the spring. Traders usually describe it as a breakout above the range. Pullbacks from it form the last point of support (LPS).
- **SOW (Sign of Weakness):** This is the distribution counterpart. It appears when price breaks below range support, often on expanding volume, and suggests distribution is nearing completion.
- **Upthrust/UTAD:** The distribution equivalent of the spring is a false breakout above resistance, which traps buyers. Upthrust After Distribution (UTAD) is usually described as a final bull trap before the decline.
- **Schematics:** Accumulation and distribution are each split into phases A through E. Distribution mirrors accumulation in reverse.

Sources also note that Wyckoff analysis works best on higher timeframes and that not every range is a Wyckoff pattern. For the original methodology, check Wyckoff Analytics directly, since this search didn't reach its materials.

**Core structure:** The pattern is a reversal setup that looks much like head and shoulders. One source describes it as forming at the end of a trend, where price makes a new high (or low) in the trend's direction and then breaks the prior swing the other way (fxopen.com). Several sources describe the QML as the level drawn at the left shoulder, with the head sweeping past it (TradingView scripts).

**Entry and targets:** Entry rules vary. One source says a bullish QM buy can be opened once the second shoulder appears, with take-profit at the peak between head and right shoulder and a stop at the head (fxopen.com). Another says that on the inverse head-and-shoulders form, the common approach waits for a break above the neckline instead of entering on the second shoulder (fxopen.com).

**Institutional/SMC overlay:** The "institutional" version adds order blocks, liquidity voids, breakers, and fair value gaps to the basic pattern (TradingView idea). A related concept is the failure-to-return (FTR) retest of the QML level (TradingView idea).

**Caveats:** Capital.com notes that the pattern has no fixed formula and can be read differently depending on how swing points are defined (capital.com). Sources also recommend confirmation from volume or momentum indicators rather than relying on the pattern alone (fxopen.com/CME reference).

The search covered the core concepts well but returned nothing specific on ATAS, Bookmap, or Jigsaw, so I can't speak to those platforms from these results.

**Core concepts**

- **Footprint charts:** A footprint chart shows traded volume at each price, split into aggressive buyers and sellers. NinjaTrader calls these volumetric bars and says they help identify imbalance, absorption, and exhaustion in real time.
- **Imbalances:** Diagonal imbalances compare the ask volume at one price with the bid volume one level below. Stacked imbalances, where several consecutive levels are imbalanced in the same direction, are treated as a stronger signal. One source notes that a single imbalance gives information but context matters more.
- **Absorption:** This is when aggressive orders hit a large resting limit order and fail to move price. One source describes it as aggressive buyers or sellers get absorbed by larger limit orders.
- **Delta divergence:** Price and delta move in opposite directions. Bullish delta divergence appears when delta is negative but price holds or rises, which suggests buyers are absorbing selling.
- **Cumulative delta (CVD):** This is the running total of delta across bars and is often analysed alongside divergence.

**Practical caveat**

Several sources stress combining signals. One warns that an imbalance without a level, or absorption without a delta shift, is a coin flip.

**Platform gap**

The results include MetaTrader 5 and TradingView tools, along with NinjaTrader's guides. None covered ATAS, Bookmap, or Jigsaw. A follow-up search on those three platforms' footprint or order flow documentation would be needed to compare them.

The search turned up good coverage of volume profile basics, but little on TPO (Market Profile) itself.

**Core concepts**
- **POC:** The price level where the most volume traded in the selected range. One source calls it fair value that often acts as a magnet for price. The Point of Control (POC) is the price level with the highest traded volume during the period. This represents the "fair value" where most market participants agreed to trade, and often acts as a magnet for price.
- **Value area:** The band where most volume traded, usually about 70%. One source puts it at 70% of the volume or trades. TradingView's method starts at the POC and adds the rows above and below it until the target volume is reached (see the row with the highest total volume), and its VAH and VAL are the top and bottom of that band (the highest price level within the value area, and the lowest price level within the value area).
- **HVN and LVN:** HVNs tend to mark accepted zones, while LVNs mark thin areas where price can move faster. HVNs often mark accepted zones, while LVNs often mark thinner zones where price may move faster. Traders also use LVNs as the opposite case: when price approaches a previous LVN, the market is much more likely to rally through or bounce off of that price level.

**Trading use**
Sources describe rotation within value as the base case in balanced markets, with directional moves more likely when value migrates. One guide describes a balanced profile as a bell curve, while an imbalanced one is elongated or skewed. The sources also flag limits: one notes that high volume at a level doesn't always mean support, so context matters.

**TPO / Market Profile**
TradingView's documentation covers TPO mainly as a set of stats. It defines POC for the TPO, and counts such as TPO above POC: The total number of blocks in this profile above the point of control. The search didn't return a full TPO tutorial covering letter-block construction or initial balance, so a dedicated Market Profile guide would be needed for that.

Source quality varies. Several results are trader blogs or crypto-broker tutorials, so treat specific trading rules as opinion rather than established fact.

The search didn't return any Elliott Wave International (EWI) pages, so these results come from third-party trading sites and educational documents. None of them is an official EWI source.

**Basic structure:** Elliott Wave describes a complete sequence of eight waves. According to Elliott, this complete sequence is divided into two distinct phases: the impulse phase and the corrective phase. Impulse waves have five subwaves and corrective waves have three. No matter how big or small the wave degree, impulse waves take on a 5-wave sequence and corrective waves take on a 3-wave sequence.

**Rules vs. guidelines:** Sources consistently separate hard rules from softer guidelines. One source says there are many guidelines, but only three HARD rules. These are unbreakable. Guidelines should hold true most of the time, not necessarily all of the time. The commonly cited impulse rules are:
- Wave 2 does not retrace beyond the origin of wave 1.
- Wave 4 does not enter the price territory of wave 1.
- Wave 3 is never the shortest of waves 1, 3 and 5 (the rule as stated in one eSignal manual).

**Guidelines and probability:** One source holds that the more Guidelines obeyed by an Elliott pattern, the higher its "rating" or "probability" of being correct. A commonly cited impulse guideline is that wave 3 often exceeds the pivot of wave 1.

**Corrective patterns:** Common types include zigzags, flats, and triangles. A zigzag is a three wave structure labeled A-B-C, generally moving counter to the larger trend. Combinations, such as a zigzag and a flat, a flat and a flat, a flat and a triangle, are also recognized.

**Caveat:** These sources vary in rule sets and terminology, and some are trading-platform posts or user-generated content. For EWI's own definitions, a direct search for their site or their published rules would be needed.

لقيت مصادر نصية لكل الفجوات، بس **ماحمّلتش أي صورة**، ومفيش صفحة Pinterest ظهرت في البحث. الملف: `data/x77/sources_2026-11-20.md`.

**المصادر لكل فجوة:**
- **Gann:** [TradingView Gann Box](https://www.tradingview.com/education/gannbox)، [TitanFX](https://research.titanfx.com/technical-analysis/other-indicators/what-is-gann-theory)، وكتاب [Breakthrough Strategies ف12](https://www.oreilly.com/library/view/breakthrough-strategies-for/9781118716014/OEBPS/c12.htm). Gann مش مثبت باستقلالية، فعامله كمرجع تاريخي بس.
- **Elliott:** [قواعد وإرشادات على TradingView](https://it.tradingview.com/chart/BTCUSD/xepjxoEQ-A-Comprehensive-Guide-to-Elliott-Wave-Rules-Guidelines)، و[O'Reilly Mastering Elliott Wave](https://www.oreilly.com/library/view/mastering-elliott-wave/9781118235157/xhtml/sec18.html)، و[eSignal manual](https://www.esignal.com/publicdocs/eSignal_Manual_ch9.pdf).
- **Wyckoff:** [CMC Markets](https://www.cmcmarkets.com/en-gb/trading-strategy/what-is-wyckoff-method)، و[TradingView](https://www.tradingview.com/education/wyckoffmethod)، و[Binance Academy](https://www.binance.com/ro/academy/articles/the-wyckoff-method-explained). فيها شرح spring وSOS وSOW.
- **QM/QML:** [FXOpen](https://fxopen.com/blog/en/quasimodo-trading-pattern/)، و[Capital.com](https://capital.com/en-int/learn/trading-strategies/quasimodo-pattern). ملاحظة: مفيش تعريف رقمي موحد.
- **Order flow / footprint / absorption:** [NinjaTrader footprint guide](https://ninjatrader.com/futures/blogs/footprint-charts-guide/)، و[MQL5 footprint](https://www.mql5.com/en/blogs/post/778690). الشرح فيه imbalance وabsorption وdelta divergence وCVD.
- **Volume profile:** [TradingView](https://www.tradingview.com/support/solutions/43000502040-volume-profile)، و[NinjaTrader part 1](https://ninjatrader.com/futures/blogs/how-to-trade-with-volume-profile-part-1/).

**الناقص (ماتلقاش مصدر رسمي في البحث):**
- Elliott Wave International
- Wyckoff Analytics
- RoadToMarket (مصدر شيت QM)
- ATAS وBookmap وJigsaw
- TPO / Market Profile الكامل (letter blocks، initial balance)

**Pinterest:** مش هقدر أجيب الصور من البحث العادي. لو تفتح صفحات Pinterest بالبراوزر، أو تبعتلي روابط صور محددة، أرجع أقراها وأحفظها. قبل أي تحميل أعرض لك اسم الملف والمصدر والحجم وتوافق.

**لم يُتحقق:** المصادر دي من نتائج البحث بس، ماتفتحتش كل الصفحات، ومعظمها مدونات تداول. مفيش حاجة من دي متثبتة في الكود.

Richard D. Wyckoff
His Method and Story
…all the fluctuations in the market and in all the various stocks should be studied as if they were the result of one man’s operations. Let us call him the Composite Man, who, in theory, sits behind the scenes and manipulates the stocks to your disadvantage if you do not understand the game as he plays it; and to your great profit if you do understand it.
(The Richard D. Wyckoff Course in Stock Market Science and Technique, section 9, p. 1-2)

Richard Demille Wyckoff (1873–1934) was an early 20th-century pioneer in the technical approach to studying the stock market. He is considered one of the five “titans” of technical analysis, along with Dow, Gann, Elliott and Merrill. At age 15, he took a job as a stock runner for a New York brokerage. While still in his 20s, he became the head of his own firm. He also founded and, for nearly two decades, wrote and edited “The Magazine of Wall Street,” which at one point, had more than 200,000 subscribers. Wyckoff was an avid student of the markets, as well as an active tape reader and trader. He observed the market activities and campaigns of the legendary stock operators of his time, including JP Morgan and Jesse Livermore. From his observations and interviews with those big-time traders, Wyckoff developed the Wyckoff Method that codified these traders’ best practices into laws, principles and techniques of trading methodology, money management and mental discipline.

From his position, Mr. Wyckoff observed numerous retail investors being repeatedly fleeced. Consequently, he dedicated himself to instructing the public about “the real rules of the game” as played by the large interests, or “smart money.” In the 1930s, he founded a school that later became the Stock Market Institute. The school’s central offering was a course based on Wyckoff schematics and integrated the concepts that Wyckoff had learned about how to identify large operators’ accumulation and distribution of stock with how to trade in harmony with these big players. His time-tested insights are as valid today as they were when first articulated.

This article provides an overview of the Wyckoff Method and both its theoretical and practical approaches to the markets, including guidelines for identifying trade candidates and for entering long and short positions, analysis of accumulation and distribution trading ranges and an explanation of how to use Point-and-Figure (P&F) charts to identify price targets. Although this article focuses exclusively on stocks, Wyckoff’s methods can be applied to any freely traded market in which large institutional traders operate, including commodities, bonds and currencies.

A Five-Step Approach to the Market
The Wyckoff Method involves a five-step approach to stock selection and trade entry, which can be summarized as follows:
1
Determine the present position and probable future trend of the market.
Is the market consolidating or trending? Does your analysis of market structure, supply and demand indicate the direction that is likely in the near future? This assessment should help you decide whether to be in the market at all and, if so, whether to take long or short positions. Use both bar charts and P&F charts of the major market indices for Step 1.
2
Select stocks in harmony with the trend.
In an uptrend, select stocks that are stronger than the market. For instance, look for stocks that demonstrate greater percentage increases than the market during rallies and smaller decreases during reactions. In a downtrend, do the reverse – choose stocks that are weaker than the market. If you are not sure about a specific issue, drop it and move on to the next one. Use bar charts of individual stocks to compare with those of the most relevant market index for Step 2.
3
Select stocks with a “cause” that equals or exceeds your minimum objective.
A critical component of Wyckoff's trade selection and management was his unique method of identifying price targets using P&F projections for both long and short trades. In Wyckoff's fundamental law of “Cause and Effect,” the horizontal P&F count within a trading range represents the cause, while the subsequent price movement represents the effect. Therefore, if you are planning to take long positions, choose stocks that are under accumulation or re-accumulation and have built a sufficient cause to satisfy your objective. Step 3 relies on the use of P&F charts of individual stocks.
4
Determine the stocks' readiness to move.
Apply the nine tests for buying or for selling (described below). For instance, in a trading range after a prolonged rally, does the evidence from the nine selling tests suggest that significant supply is entering the market and that a short position may be warranted? Or in an apparent accumulation trading range, do the nine buying tests indicate that supply has been successfully absorbed, as evidenced further by a low-volume spring and an even lower-volume test of that spring? Use bar charts and P&F charts of individual stocks for Step 4.
5
Time your commitment with a turn in the stock market index.
Three-quarters or more of individual issues move in harmony with the general market, so you improve the odds of a successful trade by having the power of the overall market behind it. Specific Wyckoff principles help you anticipate potential market turns, including a change of character of price action (such as the largest down-bar on the highest volume after a long uptrend), as well as manifestations of Wyckoff's three laws (see below). Put your stop-loss in place and then trail it, as appropriate, until you close out the position. Us

Source element: <main>
---
Footprint Charts:
What Drives Price Movement

See what happens inside each candle: actual volumes and the initiative of buyers and sellers at every price level

Try for free
Video
FOOTPRINT CLUSTERS
ORDER FLOW
What is a Footprint
in Trading

Footprint analytics is the foundation of volume analysis — a specialized chart that reveals how buy and sell orders are distributed at each price level.

While classic candlestick charts only show price direction. The Footprint, however, breaks down each candle into individual price levels and shows the detailed processes behind this movement:

how many trades occurred at each level;
where maximum and minimum volumes are concentrated;
who took the initiative — buyers or sellers;
whether the price encounters signs of support or resistance.
How Footprint Chart
Is Built

ATAS uses tick-by-tick exchange data to build footprint charts. This data includes the price, volume, and execution direction of each trade.
The information comes directly from exchanges and market data providers. It’s aggregated by price levels within each candle as numerical clusters.

You get:

a detailed picture of buys and sells at each price level;
data on volume distribution across price levels, helping you identify where volume supports price movement and where it no longer confirms it;
a visual representation of buyer and seller initiative inside the candle.

On the left — market sell orders matched with limit buy orders. On the right — market buy orders matched with limit sell orders.

  

ATAS offers more than 400 footprint variations, positioning it among the best footprint chart software tools available.

The footprint type determines what data appears inside each candle: Volume, Trades, Bid × Ask, and Delta. Each type offers multiple visualization modes, letting you customize the chart to fit your specific analysis needs.

Volume Footprint Chart

Shows trade volume at each price level within the candle:

Volume footprint — quickly assess volume distribution across price levels;
Delta Colored Volume — see the imbalance between buying and selling at each level;
Volume Histogram — visually identify levels with increased volume concentration;
Volume Digital Histogram — clearly compare volumes between levels;
Delta Colored Volume Histogram — visually highlight levels with pronounced buying or selling pressure;
Volume × Trades — see both volume and number of trades at each level.

Trades Footprint Chart

Shows the number of trades executed at each price level within the candle:

Trades — to assess trading intensity at a given price level;
Delta Colored Trades — to see buyer or seller dominance based on the number of trades;
Trades Histogram — to visually highlight price levels with increased trading activity;
Trades Digital Histogram — to accurately compare the number of trades between levels;
Delta Colored Trades Histogram — to highlight levels with a pronounced buyer or seller dominance by trade count.

Bid x Ask Footprint Chart

Shows buy and sell volumes at each price level within the candle:

Bid × Ask — to see how buying and selling volume is distributed across price levels;
Bid × Ask Ladder — to highlight only the side with a delta dominance at each level;
Bid × Ask Delta Colored — to quickly assess buyer or seller dominance at a given level;
Bid × Ask Histogram — to visually highlight price levels with increased volume activity;
Bid × Ask Digital Histogram — to accurately compare buy and sell volumes between levels;
Bid × Ask Volume Profile — to analyze the volume profile with a separation between buying and selling;
Bid × Ask Delta Profile — to evaluate the delta distribution across price levels in a profile format.

Delta Footprint Chart

Shows the difference between buying and selling volumes at each price level within the candle — the core concept of a volume delta footprint:

Delta — to see which side dominated at a given price level;
Delta × Volume — to evaluate delta in the context of total traded volume at the level;
Profile — to analyze the distribution of delta across price levels in a histogram format.

Heatmap Visualisation

This visualization mode represents footprint data as a color-based map of market activity.
Color intensity reflects data concentration at each price level, making it easier to read market structure at a glance without focusing on numeric values.

By Volume — highlights volume distribution across price levels and helps quickly identify areas of concentration;
By Trades — shows trading activity intensity based on the number of trades;
By Delta — visualizes buyer or seller dominance through delta-based color gradients.

This visualization works well for an initial market overview and in combination with classic footprint modes, adding a visual layer that helps identify key areas of interest more efficiently.

Volume Footprint Chart

Shows trade volume at each price level within the candle:

Volume footprint — quickly assess volume distribution across price levels;
Delta Colored Volume — see the imbalance between buying and selling at each level;
Volume Histogram — visually identify levels with increased volume concentration;
Volume Digital Histogram — clearly compare volumes between levels;
Delta Colored Volume Histogram — visually highlight levels with pronounced buying or selling pressure;
Volume × Trades — see both volume and number of trades at each level.

Trades Footprint Chart

Shows the number of trades executed at each price level within the candle:

Trades — to assess trading intensity at a given price level;
Delta Colored Trades — to see buyer or seller dominance based on the number of trades;
Trades Histogram — to visually highlight price levels with increased trading activity;
Trades Digital Histogram — to accurately compare the number of trades between levels;
Delta Colored Trades Histogram — to highlight levels with a pronounced buyer or seller dominance by trade count.

Bid x Ask Footprint Chart

<details><summary>نتيجة أداة (94297 حرف)</summary>

```
# Imagine — Visual Creation Suite

## Modules
Call read_me again with the modules parameter to load detailed guidance:
- `diagram` — SVG flowcharts, structural diagrams, illustrative diagrams
- `mockup` — UI mockups, forms, cards, dashboards
- `interactive` — interactive explainers with controls
- `chart` — charts, data analysis, geographic maps (Chart.js, D3 choropleth)
- `art` — illustration and generative art
Pick the closest fit. The module includes all relevant design guidance.

**Complexity budget — hard limits:**
- Box subtitles: ≤5 words. Detail goes in click-through (`sendPrompt`) or the prose below — not the box.
- Colors: ≤2 ramps per diagram. If colors encode meaning (states, tiers), add a 1-line legend. Otherwise use one neutral ramp.
- Horizontal tier: ≤4 boxes at full width (~140px each). 5+ boxes → shrink to ≤110px OR wrap to 2 rows OR split into overview + detail diagrams.

If you catch yourself writing "click to learn more" in prose, the diagram itself must ACTUALLY be sparse. Don't promise brevity then front-load everything.

**Accessibility:** For HTML widgets, begin with a visually-hidden `<h2 class="sr-only">` containing a one-sentence summary of the visualization for screen-reader users. (SVG widgets use `role="img"` with `<title>` and `<desc>` instead — see SVG setup.)

You create rich visual content — SVG diagrams/illustrations and HTML interactive widgets — that renders inline in conversation. The best output feels like a natural extension of the chat.

## Core Design System

These rules apply to ALL use cases.

### Philosophy
- **Seamless**: Users shouldn't notice where claude.ai ends and your widget begins.
- **Flat**: No gradients, mesh backgrounds, noise textures, or decorative effects. Clean flat surfaces.
- **Compact**: Show the essential inline. Explain the rest in text.
- **Text goes in your response, visuals go in the tool** — All explanatory text, descriptions, introductions, and summaries must be written as normal response text OUTSIDE the tool call. The tool output should contain ONLY the visual element (diagram, chart, interactive widget). Never put paragraphs of explanation, section headings, or descriptive prose inside the HTML/SVG. If the user asks "explain X", write the explanation in your response and use the tool only for the visual that accompanies it.

### Streaming
Output streams token-by-token. Structure code so useful content appears early.
- **HTML**: `<style>` (short) → content HTML → `<script>` last.
- **SVG**: `<defs>` (markers) → visual elements immediately.
- Prefer inline `style="..."` over `<style>` blocks — inputs/controls must look correct mid-stream.
- Keep `<style>` under ~15 lines. Interactive widgets with inputs and sliders need more style rules — that's fine, but don't bloat with decorative CSS.
- Gradients, shadows, and blur flash during streaming DOM diffs. Use solid flat fills instead.

### Rules
- No `<!-- comments -->` or `/* comments */` (waste tokens, break streaming)
- No font-size below 11px
- No emoji. Icons = Tabler **outline** webfont (5800+, already loaded): `<i class="ti ti-home"></i>`. Outline only — never use `-filled` suffixes (`ti-heart-filled` etc. are not loaded and will render blank). Inherits color + font-size from parent. Decorative icons get `aria-hidden="true"`; icon-only buttons get `aria-label`. Common: ti-home ti-settings ti-user ti-search ti-x ti-check ti-plus ti-trash ti-edit ti-download ti-upload ti-file ti-folder ti-chart-bar ti-calendar ti-clock ti-arrow-right ti-arrow-left ti-chevron-down ti-external-link ti-copy ti-refresh ti-player-play ti-player-pause ti-heart ti-star ti-bell ti-mail ti-lock ti-eye ti-menu-2. Don't hand-draw icon SVG paths.
- No gradients, drop shadows, blur, glow, or neon effects
- No dark/colored backgrounds on outer containers (transparent only — host provides the bg)
- **Typography**: The default font is the person's Interface font, usually Anthropic Sans. For the rare editorial/blockquote moment, use `font-family: var(--font-voice)`.
- **Headings**: h1 = 22px, h2 = 18px, h3 = 16px — all `font-weight: 500`. Heading color is pre-set to `var(--text-primary)` — don't override it. Body text = 16px, weight 400, `line-height: 1.7`. **Two weights only: 400 regular, 500 bold.** Never use 600 or 700 — they look heavy against the host UI.
- **Sentence case** always. Never Title Case, never ALL CAPS. This applies everywhere including SVG text labels and diagram headings.
- **No mid-sentence bolding**, including in your response text around the tool call. Entity names, class names, function names go in `code style` not **bold**. Bold is for headings and labels only.
- The widget container is `display: block; width: 100%`. Your HTML fills it naturally — no wrapper div needed. Just start with your content directly. If you want vertical breathing room, add `padding: 1rem 0` on your first element.
- Never use `position: fixed` — the iframe viewport sizes itself to your in-flow content height, so fixed-positioned elements (modals, overlays, tooltips) collapse it to `min-height: 100px`. For modal/overlay mockups: wrap everything in a normal-flow `<div style="min-height: 400px; background: rgba(0,0,0,0.45); display: flex; align-items: center; justify-content: center;">` and put the modal inside — it's a faux viewport that actually contributes layout height.
- **Fullscreen / expand buttons**: there is no fullscreen. Never call any element's `requestFullscreen()`, never call `document.exitFullscreen()`, and never key state on `document.fullscreenElement` (the API is dead inside the widget iframe and `fullscreenElement` stays null forever, so a label or branch keyed on it is permanently stuck), never call display-mode host APIs, and never fake fullscreen by restyling your container to viewport size — all of these produce dead or broken controls on at least one platform. If the user asks for a fullscreen or expand button, implement an expanded-layout toggle instead: a synchronous CSS class flip on in-flow content with explicit px sizes (taller chart, roomier controls), updating the button label in the same handler — no async, no awaits, symmetric enter/exit. Mutate the existing elements' classes and text; never rebuild the control's DOM with innerHTML (replacement silently drops event listeners and leaves a dead button). The host resizes to fit your content automatically.
- No DOCTYPE, `<html>`, `<head>`, or `<body>` — just content fragments.
- When placing text on a colored background (badges, pills, cards, tags), use the darkest shade from that same color family for the text — never plain black or generic gray.
- **Corners**: use `border-radius: var(--radius)` for controls, `12px` for cards. In SVG, `rx="4"` is the default — larger values make pills, use only when you mean a pill.
- **No rounded corners on single-sided borders** — if using `border-left` or `border-top` accents, set `border-radius: 0`. Rounded corners only work with full borders on all sides.
- **No titles or prose inside the tool output** — see Philosophy above.
- **Icon sizing**: Tabler `<i class="ti …">` sizes with `font-size` — 16–20px inline, 24px max decorative. For one-off inline SVG icons, set `width`/`height` explicitly (same limits).
- No tabs, carousels, or `display: none` sections during streaming — hidden content streams invisibly. Show all content stacked vertically. (Post-streaming JS-driven steppers are fine — see Illustrative/Interactive sections.)
- No nested scrolling — auto-fit height.
- **Validate input in interactive widgets.** Any widget that collects user input before acting on it (quiz answers, text boxes, form fields) must check that input in its submit handler: if a required field is empty or invalid, show a clear inline error next to the control (13px `color: var(--text-danger)` text, e.g. "Enter an answer first") and stop — do not advance to the next step, reveal the answer, or otherwise proceed until the input is valid. Clear the error as soon as the user edits the field.
- Scripts execute after streaming — load libraries via `<script src="https://cdnjs.cloudflare.com/ajax/libs/...">` (UMD globals), then use the global in a plain `<script>` that follows. The library `<script src>` tag must come BEFORE any inline script that uses its global — never call a library from code that appears above its `<script src>` tag.
- **CDN allowlist (CSP-enforced)**: external resources may ONLY load from `cdnjs.cloudflare.com`, `esm.sh`, `cdn.jsdelivr.net`, `unpkg.com`, `fonts.googleapis.com`, `fonts.gstatic.com`. All other origins are blocked by the sandbox — the request silently fails.

### CSS Variables
**Surfaces**: `--surface-2` (white), `--surface-1` (the page's own color), `--surface-0` (a step darker than the page); `--bg-neutral` (gray tint that shows on any page); role tints `--bg-{accent,danger,success,warning}`
**Text**: `--text-primary` (black), `--text-secondary` (muted), `--text-muted` (hints); role `--text-{accent,danger,success,warning}`
**Borders**: `--border` (default hairline), `--border-strong` (hover), `--border-stronger`; role `--border-{accent,danger,success,warning}`
**Typography**: `--font-sans`, `--font-voice` (serif), `--font-mono`
**Layout**: `--radius` (8px), `--pad-{sm,md,lg,xl}`, `--gap-{xs,sm,md,lg,xl}`; for larger corners use literal `12px`/`16px`
All auto-adapt to light/dark mode. For custom colors in HTML, use CSS variables.

**Dark mode is mandatory** — every color must work in both modes:
- In SVG: use the pre-built color classes (`c-blue`, `c-teal`, `c-amber`, etc.) for colored nodes — they handle light/dark mode automatically. Never write `<style>` blocks for colors.
- In SVG: every `<text>` element needs a class (`t`, `ts`, `th`) — never omit fill or use `fill="inherit"`. Inside a `c-{color}` parent, text classes auto-adjust to the ramp.
- In HTML: always use CSS variables (--text-primary, --text-secondary) for text. Never hardcode colors like color: #333 — invisible in dark mode.
- Mental test: if the background were near-black, would every text element still be readable?

### sendPrompt(text)
A global function that sends a message to chat as if the user typed it. Use it when the user's next step benefits from Claude thinking. Handle filtering, sorting, toggling, and calculations in JS instead.

### Links
`<a href="https://...">` just works — clicks are intercepted and open the host's link-confirmation dialog. Or call `openLink(url)` directly.

## When nothing fits
Pick the closest use case below and adapt. When nothing fits cleanly:
- Default to editorial layout if the content is explanatory
- Default to card layout if the content is a bounded object
- All core design system rules still apply
- Use `sendPrompt()` for any action that benefits from Claude thinking


## Color palette

9 color ramps, each with 7 stops from lightest to darkest. 50 = lightest fill, 100-200 = light fills, 400 = mid tones, 600 = strong/border, 800-900 = text on light fills.

| Class | Ramp | 50 (lightest) | 100 | 200 | 400 | 600 | 800 | 900 (darkest) |
|-------|------|------|-----|-----|-----|-----|-----|------|
| `c-purple` | Purple | #EEEDFE | #CECBF6 | #AFA9EC | #7F77DD | #534AB7 | #3C3489 | #26215C |
| `c-teal` | Teal | #E1F5EE | #9FE1CB | #5DCAA5 | #1D9E75 | #0F6E56 | #085041 | #04342C |
| `c-coral` | Coral | #FAECE7 | #F5C4B3 | #F0997B | #D85A30 | #993C1D | #712B13 | #4A1B0C |
| `c-pink` | Pink | #FBEAF0 | #F4C0D1 | #ED93B1 | #D4537E | #993556 | #72243E | #4B1528 |
| `c-gray` | Gray | #F1EFE8 | #D3D1C7 | #B4B2A9 | #888780 | #5F5E5A | #444441 | #2C2C2A |
| `c-blue` | Blue | #E6F1FB | #B5D4F4 | #85B7EB | #378ADD | #185FA5 | #0C447C | #042C53 |
| `c-green` | Green | #EAF3DE | #C0DD97 | #97C459 | #639922 | #3B6D11 | #27500A | #173404 |
| `c-amber` | Amber | #FAEEDA | #FAC775 | #EF9F27 | #BA7517 | #854F0B | #633806 | #412402 |
| `c-red` | Red | #FCEBEB | #F7C1C1 | #F09595 | #E24B4A | #A32D2D | #791F1F | #501313 |

**How to assign colors**: Color should encode meaning, not sequence. Don't cycle through colors like a rainbow (step 1 = blue, step 2 = amber, step 3 = red...). Instead:
- Group nodes by **category** — all nodes of the same type share one color. E.g. in a vaccine diagram: all immune cells = purple, all pathogens = coral, all outcomes = teal.
- For illustrative diagrams, map colors to **physical properties** — warm ramps for heat/energy, cool for cold/calm, green for organic, gray for structural/inert.
- Use **gray for neutral/structural** nodes (start, end, generic steps).
- Use **2-3 colors per diagram**, not 6+. More colors = more visual noise. A diagram with gray + purple + teal is cleaner than one using every ramp.
- **Prefer purple, teal, coral, pink** for general diagram categories. Reserve blue, green, amber, and red for cases where the node genuinely represents an informational, success, warning, or error concept — those colors carry strong semantic connotations from UI conventions. (Exception: illustrative diagrams may use blue/amber/red freely when they map to physical properties like temperature or pressure.)

**Text on colored backgrounds:** Always use the 800 or 900 stop from the same ramp as the fill. Never use black, gray, or --text-primary on colored fills. **When a box has both a title and a subtitle, they must be two different stops** — title darker (800 in light mode, 100 in dark), subtitle lighter (600 in light, 200 in dark). Same stop for both reads flat; the weight difference alone isn't enough. For example, text on Blue 50 (#E6F1FB) must use Blue 800 (#0C447C) or 900 (#042C53), not black. This applies to SVG text elements inside colored rects, and to HTML badges, pills, and labels with colored backgrounds.

**Light/dark mode quick pick** — use only stops from the table, never off-table hex values:
- **Light mode**: 50 fill + 600 stroke + **800 title / 600 subtitle**
- **Dark mode**: 800 fill + 200 stroke + **100 title / 200 subtitle**
- Apply `c-{ramp}` to a `<g>` wrapping shape+text, or directly to a `<rect>`/`<circle>`/`<ellipse>`. Never to `<path>` — paths don't get ramp fill. For colored connector strokes use inline `stroke="#..."` (any mid-ramp hex works in both modes). Dark mode is automatic for ramp classes. Available: c-gray, c-blue, c-red, c-amber, c-green, c-teal, c-purple, c-coral, c-pink.

For status/semantic meaning in UI (success, warning, danger) use CSS variables. For categorical coloring in both diagrams and UI, use these ramps.


## SVG setup

**ViewBox safety checklist** — before finalizing any SVG, verify:
1. Find your lowest element: max(y + height) across all rects, max(y) across all text baselines.
2. Set viewBox height = that value + 40px buffer.
3. Find your rightmost element: max(x + width) across all rects. All content must stay within x=0 to x=680.
4. For text with text-anchor="end", the text extends LEFT from x. If x=118 and text is 200px wide, it starts at x=-82 — outside the viewBox. Increase x or use text-anchor="start".
5. Never use negative x or y coordinates. The viewBox starts at 0,0.
6. **No unintentional overlaps.** For every pair of elements that aren't meant to layer (label-on-label, label-on-arrow, box-on-box, callout-on-shape), check their bounding boxes do not intersect. The only allowed overlaps are deliberate: a label centered inside its own box, an arrowhead touching the box it points to, a highlight rect behind the thing it highlights. If two unrelated elements would collide, move one — shorten the label, shift the y, add a row. A diagram with crossed labels reads as broken regardless of how good the content is.
7. Flowcharts/structural only: for every pair of boxes in the same row, check that the left box's (x + width) is less than the right box's x by at least 20px. If four 160px boxes plus three 20px gaps sum to more than 640px, the row doesn't fit — shrink the boxes or cut the subtitles, don't let them overlap.

**SVG setup**: `<svg width="100%" viewBox="0 0 680 H" role="img"><title>…</title><desc>…</desc>…` — 680px wide, flexible height. The root `<svg>` MUST carry `role="img"` with `<title>` and `<desc>` as its first children so screen readers can announce what the diagram shows. Set H to fit content tightly — the last element's bottom edge + 40px padding. Don't leave excess empty space below the content. Safe area: x=40 to x=640, y=40 to y=(H-40). Background transparent. **Do not wrap the SVG in a container `<div>` with a background color** — the widget host already provides the card container and background. Output the raw `<svg>` element directly.

**The 680 in viewBox is load-bearing — do not change it.** It matches the widget container width so SVG coordinate units render 1:1 with CSS pixels. With `width="100%"`, the browser scales the entire coordinate space to fit the container: `viewBox="0 0 476 H"` in a 680px container scales everything by 680/476 = 1.43×, so your `class="th"` 14px text renders at ~20px. The font calibration table below and all "text fits in box" math assume 1:1. If your diagram content is naturally narrow, **keep viewBox width at 680 and center the content** (e.g. content spans x=240..440) — do not shrink the viewBox to hug the content. This applies equally to inline SVGs inside HTML steppers and widgets: same `viewBox="0 0 680 H"`, same 1:1 guarantee.

**viewBox height:** After layout, find max_y (bottom-most point of any shape, including text baselines + 4px descent). Set viewBox height = max_y + 20. Don't guess.

**text-anchor='end' at x<60 is risky** — the longest label will extend left past x=0. Use text-anchor='start' and right-align the column instead, or check: label_chars × 8 < anchor_x.

**One SVG per tool call** — each call must contain exactly one <svg> element. Never leave an abandoned or partial SVG in the output. If your first attempt has problems, replace it entirely — do not append a corrected version after the broken one.

**Style rules for all diagrams**:
- Every `<text>` element must carry one of the pre-built classes (`t`, `ts`, `th`). An unclassed `<text>` inherits the default sans font, which is the tell that you forgot the class.
- Use only two font sizes: 14px for node/region labels (class="t" or "th"), 12px for subtitles, descriptions, and arrow labels (class="ts"). No other sizes.
- No decorative step numbers, large numbering, or oversized headings outside boxes.
- No icons or illustrations inside boxes — text only. (Exception: illustrative diagrams may use simple shape-based indicators inside drawn objects — see below.)
- Sentence case on all labels.

**Font size calibration for diagram text labels** - Here's csv table to give you better sense of the Anthropic Sans font rendering width:
```csv
text, chars length, font-weight, font-size, rendered width
Authentication Service, chars: 22, font-weight: 500, font-size: 14px, width: 167px
Background Job Processor, chars: 24, font-weight: 500, font-size: 14px, width: 201px
Detects and validates incoming tokens, chars: 37, font-weight: 400, font-size: 14px, width: 279px
forwards request to, chars: 19, font-weight: 400, font-size: 12px, width: 123px
1234567890, chars: 10, font-weight: 400, font-size: 12px, width: 72px
データベースサーバー接続, chars: 12, font-weight: 400, font-size: 14px, width: 181px
```

Before placing text in a box, check: does (text width + 2×padding) fit the container?

**SVG `<text>` never auto-wraps.** Every line break needs an explicit `<tspan x="..." dy="1.2em">`. If your subtitle is long enough to need wrapping, it's too long — shorten it (see complexity budget).

**Example check**: You want to put "Glucose (C₆H₁₂O₆)" in a rounded rect. The text is 20 characters at 14px ≈ 180px wide. Add 2×24px padding = 228px minimum box width. If your rect is only 160px wide, the text WILL overflow — either shorten the label (e.g. just "Glucose") or widen the box. Subscript characters like ₆ and ₁₂ still take horizontal space — count them.

**Pre-built classes** (already loaded in SVG widget):
- `class="t"` = sans 14px primary, `class="ts"` = sans 12px secondary, `class="th"` = sans 14px medium (500)
- `class="box"` = neutral rect (`--surface-1` fill, `--border-strong` stroke)
- `class="node"` = clickable group with hover effect (cursor pointer, slight dim on hover)
- `class="arr"` = arrow line (1.5px, open chevron head)
- `class="leader"` = dashed leader line (tertiary stroke, 0.5px, dashed)
- `class="c-{ramp}"` = colored node (c-blue, c-teal, c-amber, c-green, c-red, c-purple, c-coral, c-pink, c-gray). Apply to `<g>` or shape element (rect/circle/ellipse), NOT to paths. Sets fill+stroke on shapes, auto-adjusts child `t`/`ts`/`th`, dark mode automatic.

**c-{ramp} nesting:** These classes use direct-child selectors (`>`). Nest a `<g>` inside a `<g class="c-blue">` and the inner shapes become grandchildren — they lose the fill and render BLACK (SVG default). Put `c-*` on the innermost group holding the shapes, or on the shapes directly. If you need click handlers, put `onclick` on the `c-*` group itself, not a wrapper.

- Short aliases: `var(--p)`, `var(--s)`, `var(--t)`, `var(--bg2)`, `var(--b)`
- Arrow marker: always include this `<defs>` at the start of every SVG:
  `<defs><marker id="arrow" viewBox="0 0 10 10" refX="8" refY="5" markerWidth="6" markerHeight="6" orient="auto-start-reverse"><path d="M2 1L8 5L2 9" fill="none" stroke="context-stroke" stroke-width="1.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"/></marker></defs>`
  Then use `marker-end="url(#arrow)"` on lines. The head uses `context-stroke`, so it inherits the colour of whichever line it sits on — a dashed green line gets a green head, a grey line gets a grey head. Never a colour mismatch. Do not add filters or extra markers to `<defs>`. `<pattern>` fills are allowed when used as a secondary encoding for categorical data — keep them subtle (thin hatching, sparse dots). Never rely on color alone to distinguish categories; pair each color with a secondary visual cue (hatching, dash pattern, or shape). Illustrative diagrams may add a single `<clipPath>` or `<linearGradient>` (see Illustrative section).

**Minimize standalone labels.** Every `<text>` element must be inside a box (title or ≤5-word subtitle) or in the legend. Arrow labels are usually unnecessary — if the arrow's meaning isn't obvious from its source + target, put it in the box subtitle or in prose below. Labels floating in space collide with things and are ambiguous.

**Stroke width:** Use 0.5px strokes for diagram borders and edges — not 1px or 2px. Thin strokes feel more refined.

**Connector paths need `fill="none"`.** SVG defaults to `fill: black` — a curved connector without `fill="none"` renders as a huge black shape instead of a clean line. Every `<path>` or `<polyline>` used as a connector/arrow MUST have `fill="none"`. Only set fill on shapes meant to be filled (rects, circles, polygons).

**Rect rounding:** `rx="4"` for subtle corners. `rx="8"` max for emphasized rounding. `rx` ≥ half the height = pill shape — deliberate only.

**Schematic containers use dashed rects with a label.** Don't draw literal shapes (organelle ovals, cloud outlines, server tower icons) — the diagram is a schema, not an illustration. A dashed `<rect>` labeled "Reactor vessel" reads cleaner than an `<ellipse>` that clips content.

**Lines stop at component edges.** When a line meets a component (wire into a bulb, edge into a node), draw it as segments that stop at the boundary — never draw through and rely on a fill to hide the line. The background color is not guaranteed; any occluding fill is a coupling. Compute the stop/start coordinates from the component's position and size.

**Physical-color scenes (sky, water, grass, skin, materials):** Use ALL hardcoded hex — never mix with `c-*` theme classes. The scene should not invert in dark mode. If you need a dark variant, key it on the app theme: `[data-mode="dark"] .scene` for the normal case, plus `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark) { :root:not([data-mode]) .scene }` as the OS fallback for when no app theme was provided (the widget root carries `data-mode`; a bare media query alone would follow the OS even when the claude.ai setting disagrees) — this is the one place dark variants for hardcoded colors are allowed. Mixing hardcoded backgrounds with theme-responsive `c-*` foreground breaks: half inverts, half doesn't.

**No rotated text**. `<defs>` may contain the arrow marker, a `<clipPath>`, subtle `<pattern>` fills used as a secondary visual cue alongside color for categorical data, and — in illustrative diagrams only — a single `<linearGradient>`. Nothing else: no filters, no extra markers.


## Diagram types
*"Explain how compound interest works" / "How does a process scheduler work"*

**Two rules that cause most diagram failures — check these before writing each arrow and each box:**
1. **Arrow intersection check**: before writing any `<line>` or `<path>`, trace its coordinates against every box you've already placed. If the line crosses any rect's interior (not just its source/target), it will visibly slash through that box — use an L-shaped `<path>` detour instead. This applies to arrows crossing labels too.
2. **Box width from longest label**: before writing a `<rect>`, find its longest child text (usually the subtitle). `rect_width = max(title_chars × 8, subtitle_chars × 7) + 24`. A 100px-wide box holds at most a 10-char subtitle. If your subtitle is "Files, APIs, streams" (20 chars), the box needs 164px minimum — 100px will visibly overflow.

**Tier packing:** Compute total width BEFORE placing. Example — 4 pub/sub consumer boxes:
- WRONG: x=40,160,260,360 w=160 → 40-60px overlaps (4×160=640 > 480 available)
- RIGHT: x=50,200,350,500 w=130 gap=20 → fits (4×130 + 3×20 = 580 ≤ 590 safe width; right edge at 630 ≤ 640)
Work bottom-up for trees: size leaf tier first, parent width ≥ sum of children.

**Diagrams are the hardest use case** — they have the highest failure rate due to precise coordinate math. Common mistakes: viewBox too small (content clipped), arrows through unrelated boxes, labels on arrow lines, text past viewBox edges. For illustrative diagrams, also watch for: shapes extending outside the viewBox, overlapping labels that obscure the drawing, and color choices that don't map intuitively to the physical properties being shown. Double-check coordinates before finalizing.

Use SVG for diagrams. The widget automatically wraps SVG output in a card.

**Pick the right diagram type.** The decision is about *intent*, not subject matter. Ask: is the user trying to *document* this, or *understand* it?

**Reference diagrams** — the user wants a map they can point at. Precision matters more than feeling. Boxes, labels, arrows, containment. These are the diagrams you'd find in documentation.
- **Flowchart** — steps in sequence, decisions branching, data transforming. Good for: approval workflows, request lifecycles, build pipelines, "what happens when I click submit". Trigger phrases: *"walk me through the process"*, *"what are the steps"*, *"what's the flow"*.
- **Structural diagram** — things inside other things. Good for: file systems (blocks in inodes in partitions), VPC/subnet/instance, "what's inside a cell". Trigger phrases: *"what's the architecture"*, *"how is this organised"*, *"where does X live"*.

**Intuition diagrams** — the user wants to *feel* how something works. The goal isn't a correct map, it's the right mental model. These should look nothing like a flowchart. The subject doesn't need a physical form — it needs a *visual metaphor*.
- **Illustrative diagram** — draw the mechanism. Physical things get cross-sections (water heaters, engines, lungs). Abstract things get spatial metaphors: an LLM is a stack of layers with tokens lighting up as attention weights, gradient descent is a ball rolling down a loss surface, a hash table is a row of buckets with items falling into them, TCP is two people passing numbered envelopes. Good for: ML concepts (transformers, attention, backprop, embeddings), physics intuition, CS fundamentals (pointers, recursion, the call stack), anything where the breakthrough is *seeing* it rather than *reading* it. Trigger phrases: *"how does X actually work"*, *"explain X"*, *"I don't get X"*, *"give me an intuition for X"*.

**Route on the verb, not the noun.** Same subject, different diagram depending on what was asked:

| User says | Type | What to draw |
|---|---|---|
| "how do LLMs work" | **Illustrative** | Token row, stacked layer slabs, attention threads glowing warm between tokens. Go interactive if you can. |
| "transformer architecture" | Structural | Labelled boxes: embedding, attention heads, FFN, layer norm. |
| "how does attention work" | **Illustrative** | One query token, a fan of lines to every key, line opacity = weight. |
| "how does gradient descent work" | **Illustrative** | Contour surface, a ball, a trail of steps. Slider for learning rate. |
| "what are the training steps" | Flowchart | Forward → loss → backward → update. Boxes and arrows. |
| "how does TCP work" | **Illustrative** | Two endpoints, numbered packets in flight, an ACK returning. |
